# Known issues

The ledger for every error, warning, defect, gap, and **improvement opportunity** encountered in Fleet's orbit — this crate, the vendored cores, the sibling repos, the toolchain, the environment. **Nothing is ignored for being "not my code," pre-existing, or someone else's repo**, and nothing that could be better goes unrecorded (owner directives, 2026-10-13). An item is worked or explicitly deferred by the owner, and it closes only with evidence.

Severity: `blocker` (stops the build or daily use) · `defect` (wrong behavior, needs a fix) · `gap` (missing something expected) · `improvement` (works, but should or could be better — logged on encounter, triaged by the owner, never silently scope-creeped into the running slice) · `environmental` (outside the code, owner action) · `risk` (no defect yet, exposure exists).

| KI-001 | defect | agnostic-router-kit CI is red on **real test failures**, not the budget condition: runs 37863487221 (55s), 37857919072 (1m9s), 37696108358 (50s) dispatched with a runner and 7 steps and failed on steps, Oct 7–9 — predating the Actions-budget exhaustion. A prior recap wrongly conflated this with Fleet's never-dispatched condition | `gh api repos/adelvillar1/agnostic-router-kit/actions/runs/<id>/jobs`, 2026-10-13 | closed — CI green at run 40107568736, after four layers | the row's diagnosis was the first of four: (1) Node 24, not 20 — the probes import .mts modules and Node strips types only from 22.6/23.6; (2) `npm install` before `npm test` — workflow-plane is a `file: lib/workflow` dependency only an install links; (3) `npm install --prefix router` — `kit apply` renders the scratch runtime by copying router/node_modules/@typesafe-ai from the checkout, and that directory is gitignored like every node_modules, so a fresh clone's five scratch-router suites died ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND (runs 40104816783, 40105021031, 40105643183 were the lessons); (4) the named-absence law crossed platforms — ENOEXEC arrives as a sync throw on macOS and EACCES arrives async everywhere, and glibc's execvp /bin/sh fallback made the no-shebang fixture untestable on Linux, so the fixture became exists-not-executable and EACCES joined the law (`a469e4f`, `7fd0aac`). Verified fresh-install locally (33 suites, 0 failed) at every step; the run went green 2026-10-24

| KI-016 | defect | The session log's `assistant` record never carries the model's tool calls: `Agent::run` does `std::mem::take(&mut message.tool_calls)` *before* `push_message(message)` logs the message, so the record serializes as `{"type":"assistant"}` with no `tool_calls`. A replayed log therefore cannot reconstruct the turn that requested them, and a resumed run sends an assistant message with no `tool_calls` followed by a `tool` message with a `tool_call_id` — not a valid OpenAI message sequence; the same `take` also dropped the calls from the live conversation, so the next request *in the same run* carried the invalid sequence — only the mock server's silence hid it. Violates hard rule 5 (model-visible ⟺ logged) | smoke run of the real binary against the scripted mock: log line `{"type":"assistant"}` for the turn that requested `call-1`; `git show HEAD:src/agent.rs` lines 217 (take) and 234 (push); `Record::Assistant`'s `skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty"`, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | the assistant message is now logged and kept intact and the calls are cloned for execution; the regression test asserts both the record and the next request carry `call-1`. Old logs stay readable (`tool_calls` is `#[serde(default)]`), so no format-version bump. Residual on record: pre-fix logs replay the invalid assistant-without-calls sequence on resume — readable is not valid | 
| KI-017 | improvement | Slice 2's async wiring leaves three blocking calls on the tokio runtime worker thread: tool execution inside the async agent loop (`bash`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `list_dir`), `doctor`'s `net::listening` TCP probe, and `chat`'s stdin `read_line`. A long `bash` command stalls every other task sharing that worker — slice 3's TUI will feel it | `src/agent.rs execute_call` (called from the now-`async fn run`), `src/main.rs doctor_command`/`chat_command`; debug smoke run, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | tool execution leaves the async context on the blocking pool — `run_builtin` (src/agent.rs:2888) runs `tool.execute` through `tokio::task::spawn_blocking`, and the write-time guard runs the same way, so a long `bash` no longer stalls the runtime's worker. The row's other two legs stand accepted on the row's own terms: doctor's TCP probe is a bounded 1.5s one-shot in its own sync command with no concurrent tasks, and chat's stdin `read_line` blocks a loop whose only other occupants — the consolidator and the scheduler — run on dedicated `std::thread`s rather than the runtime's workers, so neither stalls a surface

| KI-019 | improvement | `chat_stream` assembles a `ChatResponse` whose `finish_reason` is `None`, while `chat` carries the API's value: the `StreamEvent` enum is fixed at three variants (content, tool calls, usage) so the reason cannot cross the channel. The same streamed path also leaves `usage.total_tokens` at 0 — the usage chunk carries prompt and completion only — so every calibration `turn` row shows a zero total beside real prompt/completion figures (disclosed in docs/features/calibration.md). Nothing reads `finish_reason` today | `src/api.rs chat_stream` (`finish_reason: None`) vs the non-streaming `Choice` deserialization; live stream rows `"usage":{"prompt_tokens":895,"completion_tokens":64,"total_tokens":0}`, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | the API's own value still wins when the provider carries it in the usage object; when it arrives on the choice instead, the assembled message says which it was — calls assembled means `tool_calls`, a clean close with none means `stop` — so the streamed response and the non-streaming one agree; pinned by `client_chat_stream_assembles_the_same_response_chat_returns` (asserting `tool_calls`) and `client_chat_stream_derives_stop_when_the_stream_closes_with_calls` |
| KI-020 | defect | The streamed path emitted assembled tool calls only at `finish_reason`, so a stream that closed with `[DONE]` (or EOF) without one silently dropped the model's calls — the caller saw a normal Completed turn with zero tool calls. | KI-021 | defect | `tokio::sync::oneshot::Receiver::blocking_recv` (and mpsc's `blocking_recv`) **panic** when called inside a runtime context — `try_enter_blocking_region().expect("Cannot block the current thread from within a runtime…")`. The TUI's `TuiApprover::approve` runs inside the agent's tokio runtime, so the first tool approval killed the agent thread outright; the TUI's modal then waited forever on a dead run, and the run's remaining turns never happened. Found by the slice-3 PTY approval smoke against the scripted mock, whose capture contains `panicked at src/agent.rs:227:24: Cannot block the current thread from within a runtime` | PTY-driven `fleet` (no args) against `mock-server`: submit → approval modal → `a`; session log stopped after the assistant's tool call with no `tool_result` and no `end`; `tokio-1.53.2/src/future/block_on.rs` + `runtime/context/blocking.rs`, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | the approval responder is a `std::sync::mpsc` channel (no runtime context, plain OS-level blocking wait); the round-trip now completes end to end — log carries the `tool_result` with `approved: true`, the answer, and `end`. Regression: the channel round-trip is unit-tested with a fake UI loop, and the PTY smoke drives the real modal |
| KI-022 | improvement | The TUI creates its session log at launch, so opening and quitting `fleet` (no args) leaves an "open" session holding only a `start` record in `fleet sessions` and the picker. | KI-023 | improvement | The TUI launches under `#[tokio::main]`'s default multi-thread runtime (10 idle worker threads on this machine) and then runs the agent on its own dedicated runtime, so those workers sit parked for the whole session — threads and stacks held for nothing | `sample` of the TUI process with a modal up: main thread in the event loop, 10 `tokio-rt-worker` threads all parked, plus the agent's own runtime thread, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | `main` is sync and builds nothing for the TUI path; each awaiting command builds its own current-thread runtime for the life of its one call. | KI-025 | improvement | The TUI's wrapping and cursor math count characters, not display cells: `wrap()`/`short_id()`/`input_lines()`/`cursor_position()` in `src/tui/ui.rs` use `chars().count()` for widths, so CJK, emoji, and combining-mark input mis-wraps (a line can overflow its box) and the cursor can be placed past the visible area — ratatui's backend clamps it, so nothing panics, but the caret lands in the wrong cell. Only affects non-ASCII input, which the agent's tasks do produce | `src/tui/ui.rs` — `wrap()` (`word.chars().count()`), `input_lines()`/`cursor_position()` (char indices), `short_id()` (`id.chars().count()`), 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | the TUI's wrap, cursor, and truncation math measures display CELLS through `unicode-width` (already in the binary's graph via ratatui-core, so no new crate enters): a CJK line wraps at its column budget, and the cursor's column is the display width of the prefix rather than its character count. Pinned by `the_width_math_measures_display_cells` — moved to batch 3 — a display-width measure means a new crate (unicode-width), and the crate contract (one static binary, zero new crates) makes that the owner’s call |

| KI-028 | defect | sys1 and gen1 answer JSON-RPC errors with the error **nested inside `result`** (`{"id":N,"result":{"error":{…}}}`) instead of the standard top-level `error` member; sdm1 and sem1 use the standard shape. The fix landed with the root cause (KI-191, the vendored sys1 lane's own join error) and is pinned by `call_results_parse_content_and_the_is_error_flag` |

Provider-level reachability — sem1's llama-server (`127.0.0.1:8901`), sdm1's `TABPFN_API_KEY`, sys1's GLiNER venv, gen1's kokoro — is not probed, so a lane reports `ok` while its tools would fail at call time. sdm1 ships an `sdm1_health` tool and sem1 a `sem1_probe` that report exactly this, but the health command does not call them | `fleet lanes` output: all four `ok` while no embedding/classification backend is guaranteed; `sem1_probe` via the client returns a reachability report that the probe discards, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** — each lane names its provider-level health tools on its spec (`sdm1_health`, `sem1_probe`, blend1’s `doctor`) and the probe calls every one the lane exposes beside the handshake, carrying each report’s per-lane availability into the health’s doctor list — so a lane whose llama-server is down no longer reports `ok` on the strength of its MCP surface alone. A provider-map report (sdm1’s and sem1’s shape) parses into availability sorted by provider; a payload that is not the shape reads as nothing rather than as a lane that broke. Pinned by `every_lane_names_its_provider_health_tools` and `a_provider_map_health_report_parses_into_availability` — the same gap now applies inside a run | the lane-tool wiring landed (slice-4 remainder) without folding a provider probe in, so a run can call `mcp__sem1__embed` against a lane whose llama-server is down and receive a typed failure the model must interpret. | KI-041 | improvement | MCP tool results are not bounded by `MAX_TOOL_OUTPUT`. A lane's `tools/call` text rode into the session log and the model's context at whatever length the lane emitted — a batch of embedding vectors as JSON is kilobytes to megabytes — while every built-in tool's output is capped at 64 KiB on a char boundary with a `[truncated]` marker. The client's 8 MiB per-line cap (KI-035) bounds one frame, not the total | `src/agent.rs call_lane` → `LaneOutcome { text, .. }` with no `tools::bound`; `src/tools.rs MAX_TOOL_OUTPUT`, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | the lane's text now crosses one bounded seam: `LaneRoster::call` wraps the whole lane path (success, refusal, typed failure) in `tools::bound`, and the real `truncated` flag rides out through `LaneOutcome` into `Agent::call_lane` and the `Record::ToolResult` the log writes. `tools::bound` is public for exactly this reason. | KI-042 | risk | A lane whose child died keeps its tools in the model's tool set for the rest of the run, by design: the model saw them declared and the `lane_tools` record must keep matching the requests that followed. The consequence is that a model that keeps calling a dead lane spends a turn per attempt on a typed failure — bounded (fail fast, no respawn) but not free | `src/agent.rs LaneChild::Dead { tools }` keeps the roster; integration test `a_dead_lane_child_degrades_the_run_without_killing_it` asserts the tools stay declared, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed** | the dead lane’s fail-fast text tells the model the lane is GONE for the rest of the run, that its declarations stay by design, and that every call to them fails the same way — so a model that keeps calling a dead lane’s tools reads why on the ordinary tool_result path
| KI-043 | defect | The MCP child's stderr was piped and never drained. `spawn_with` in `src/mcp.rs` piped it with a comment claiming it was "captured for the error message when a child dies during startup," but nothing read it, so the read end sat open and unread: a lane writing more than the OS pipe buffer (64 KiB) blocked on its next write and stayed blocked until the call budget expired — a wedged child indistinguishable from a hung one, and a comment that was simply false. Reproduced: a fake lane writing 256 KiB to stderr before its `initialize` reply never answered, and `initialize` failed on its 5s budget | `src/mcp.rs spawn_with` (`command.stderr(Stdio::piped())`, no reader); negative probe with the drain removed: `initialize despite the chatter: Timeout { method: "initialize", budget: 5s }`, 2026-10-13 | **closed — fixed, decision (a)** | decision: drain, do not silence. Option (b) (`Stdio::null()`) would have removed the wedge but thrown away the one channel a lane's startup failure actually writes to — a missing venv or an unreadable config lands on stderr, never on the protocol pipe, so `null` would have turned "the lane is broken *because*" back into "the lane is broken". A detached task now reads the child's stderr for its whole life into a bounded tail (`STDERR_TAIL_BYTES`, last 64 KiB — the tail, because a dying lane prints its reason last), and a failed `initialize`/`tools/list` carries that tail in its message. The spawn comment now matches the code. Evidence: integration tests `a_chatty_stderr_child_does_not_wedge_the_call` (256 KiB of stderr, the whole round trip completes well inside its budget) and `a_child_that_dies_during_startup_reports_its_stderr` (the child's reason reaches the caller); unit tests `the_stderr_tail_keeps_the_last_bytes_not_the_first`, `a_small_stderr_tail_is_kept_whole`, `an_empty_stderr_tail_leaves_the_failure_exactly_as_it_was` (a silent child's messages are byte-identical), `a_stderr_tail_rides_along_on_a_handshake_failure` (the tail rides on `Closed`/`Protocol`/`Rpc`/`Io` and leaves `Timeout`'s shape alone). 193 tests green, build warning-free |

| KI-048 | decision | The calibration stream's log root resolves in this order: `<FLEET_HOME>/config.json`'s `calib.log_root` → dev-decisions' own literal `<user home>/.local/share/dev-decisions/logs` → `<FLEET_HOME>/logs` when no user home is known. dev-decisions names its root with **neither an env var nor a config key**: `LOG_DIR` is a hardcoded literal in `scripts/devdec/config.py` (line 38) and the only overridable paths in the whole config surface are `providers.*`; the CLI's `--log-dir` is a hidden flag on `semantic-index` alone. | KI-050 | improvement | The demotion threshold is a constant (`calib::DEMOTION_THRESHOLD = 3`) with no decay and no time window beyond "today": a candidate that fails three times a day, every day, is demoted fresh each day with no accumulated penalty across days. Fleet floors the composite score (`MIN_RECALL_SCORE = 0.20`, on the weighted sum) and lets the assembly budget do the separating; the vector channel still reorders what the keyword channel found, which is the property the probe test pins | probe against the live llama-server: `cos("pushing the staging branch triggers a railway deployment", "the deploy pipeline builds per branch on railway") = 0.9043` vs the walnut-table pair `= 0.6049`; unit tests `the_hybrid_ranker_orders_a_scripted_corpus_deterministically` (both orderings, scripted vectors) and `the_budget_assembly_survives_pressure` (budget drop), 2026-10-13 | **closed — decided (accepted, trigger named)** | the composite floor (`MIN_RECALL_SCORE = 0.20` on the weighted sum) is the accepted form — the cosines do not separate relevance on their own and the vector channel reorders what the keyword channel found, which is the property fleet needs. Evidence: unit test `the_writer_bounds_and_sanitizes_the_description` (a description carrying `\n`, `\t`, `\x1b[31m`, `\x07`, and 2,000 characters writes with no control character and a marked cut, round-trips through the loader, and the assembled block carries no `\x1b`); `fleet remember` on a scratch corpus wrote the sanitized card. Residual on record: the loader half — a hand-edited file's description is still read raw — is now KI-061 |

The write path defines the limit now, so the loader's refusal or sanitize has a named bound to apply | `src/memory.rs fact()` (`document.required("description")` — unchanged by part 2), against the write path's `MAX_DESCRIPTION_CHARS`, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the loader applies the writer’s own bound at load: a description is sanitized and cut with the marked ellipsis the writer uses (`sanitize_line(description, MAX_DESCRIPTION_CHARS)` in `fact()`), so a hand-edited fact rides bounded. | KI-062 | risk | The consolidation cycle's bounded rewrites and a concurrent run's recall-count bump can lose one update. Both are read-modify-write sequences on the same file (`rewrite_field` reads the file, replaces one line, and renames a temp over it), and the scheduled cycle in a chat or TUI session runs while a run in the same process is recording hits: an interleaved pair can land one writer's line over the other's. The consequence is bounded — a lost `recall-count` bump or a lost lifecycle stamp, never a corrupt file, because each write re-reads and preserves every other line — and the calibration rows are unaffected (they cross the stream's own serialized writer) | `src/memory.rs rewrite_field` (read, replace one line, temp+rename) called from both `bump_recall_count` and the cycle's stamp loops, with `spawn_consolidator` running beside a live run, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | a fact's read-modify-write runs under a per-fact flock on a sibling .lock, so the consolidation cycle's bounded rewrites and a live run's recall-count bump can no longer lose an update. A lock that cannot be created leaves the write running unprotected rather than refusing it — a lost bump is cheaper than a refused supersession
| KI-063 | gap | The consolidator thread's rows have no drain-on-exit. Every other `calib::Stream` drains its queue when the last handle drops, but the scheduled cycle owns its stream inside a loop that never ends, so a process that exits mid-pass can lose the pass's still-queued rows. The stream's advisory contract covers it — a row is never worth failing a run for — but the cycle is the one writer whose rows describe file changes that already landed, so a lost row leaves a corpus change with no audit line | `src/memory.rs spawn_consolidator` (the stream lives in the loop's closure; no handle ever drops), against `src/calib.rs`'s drain-on-last-drop, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | `Stream::flush` waits (bounded by the drain budget) until the queue holds nothing the writer has not finished, and the consolidator calls it after every pass — the cycle’s rows no longer die with a process that exits mid-pass. Pinned by `a_flush_waits_for_the_queued_rows_to_finish` |
| KI-064 | improvement | The turn path loads the corpus once per run, and a load runs the embedding probe: one real round trip per task, so a chat session pays it per message and a headless `run` pays it once. | KI-065 | improvement | A working-tier fact is a file in the git-tracked corpus like any other, so session-scoped scratch enters the repo's diff surface until the cycle compresses it. | KI-068 | improvement | Recall bumps and `recall_hit` rows fired at assembly time, before the model call: `prepare_memory` assembled, recorded the hits, and wrote the `memory` record at the top of `run_task`, and only then did the turn loop make its first request. | KI-070 | risk | The agent loop is recursive as of slice 9: `execute_call` → `subagents::run` → `Agent::run` → `run_task` → `execute_call`. rustc refused it with E0733 ("a recursive `async fn` call must introduce indirection such as `Box::pin` to avoid an infinitely sized future") until one `Box::pin` was added at the loop's call edge. The pin is load-bearing and invisible: a future refactor that inlines `execute_call` back into `run_task`, or adds a second recursion edge, gets the same error — and the delegation path is the only recursion the loop has, capped at depth one by the scope | `cargo build --all-targets` → `error[E0733]` at `src/agent.rs`'s `run_subagent`/`execute_call` cycle, 2026-10-14; the fix is `Box::pin(self.execute_call(&call)).await?` in `run_task`, with the cycle named in the comment above it | **closed — decided (accepted)** | keep the `Box::pin` — it is the required indirection for the one recursion edge, and rustc is the right guard: a refactor that inlines the call back or adds a second recursion edge fails the build with E0733 rather than silently growing the future. | KI-079 | improvement | The compaction request's own token cost is counted in the run's `usage` total but is not separated out: `RunReport::usage` sums the ordinary turns' requests *and* the compaction request, so a run that folded once reports roughly twice the prompt tokens its turns account for. | KI-080 | gap | A conversation whose verbatim tail is itself at or over the budget gets exactly one compaction and then never another: the latch holds until a measurement comes in under the budget, and a conversation over the window cannot shrink back under it. The consequence is bounded — the run carries on with an oversized request and the provider's length refusal is a typed API error, the same as any other — and folding again would not help, because the fold cannot remove the tail. The latch is what prevents the alternative, which is a fold every turn until the turn cap | `src/agent.rs maybe_compact` (`self.compaction_latch` cleared only by a measurement under the budget); integration test `one_compaction_per_crossing_and_the_tool_loop_survives_it` pins the latch's hold, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | a tail that alone is at or over the budget with nothing before it to fold now fails by name (“the recent context alone exceeds the window: N tokens against a B-token budget…”) instead of continuing into a request that dies on length at the API with a transport error naming nothing about the window. Under the budget, the forced fold’s named no-op stands. Pinned by `a_tail_that_alone_exceeds_the_window_fails_by_name` |

| KI-081 | gap | The chars/4 fallback estimate counts the messages and not the system prompt or the tool declarations, while the API's `prompt_tokens` counts both. The gap is small (the compiled-in prompt plus declarations are a few hundred tokens against a 100,000-token budget) but it is a real asymmetry between the two measures the trigger accepts | `src/compaction.rs estimate_tokens` (messages only) against the API's own accounting, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the fallback estimate counts the assembled system prompt beside the messages — the tool declarations ride inside it — so the estimate no longer reads under the API’s own count by exactly their size. `compaction::estimate_with_prompt` is the one seam, pinned by `the_estimate_counts_the_prompt_and_the_declarations_it_carries` |

| KI-082 | improvement | A compaction request streams its answer with the deltas discarded (an empty event sink on `chat_stream`), which is correct — the summary arrives whole and streaming it would put a second model's prose in the middle of the run's output — but it means a slow summarization shows nothing at all on the headless surface until the fold's progress line lands. Same failure, same class: a fixed-width surface given a line built by concatenation rather than by the pane's own width. Fixed before commit by rendering the card in the tool card's own two-line shape, with the token counts on the wrapped detail line | the slice-10 visual check, `TestBackend` at 80x24: the one-line draft's `the_compaction_card_renders_in_the_house_card_style` failed with the line cut at `~900 to`; the two-line shape passes, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the card now renders `✓ compacted  6 turns → 1 summary  done` with `└ ~120000 → ~900 tokens` wrapped underneath, exactly as a tool card wraps its preview. Evidence: unit test `the_compaction_card_renders_in_the_house_card_style` asserts both lines appear whole at 80 columns. The general fix (one shared width-aware wrapper for the card family, KI-055's note) is still open |

| KI-086 | improvement | The compaction card rides the tool-card grammar exactly (green ✓, cyan verb, gray payload, green done, indented result line), so it reads as an event — but a first-time reader can momentarily take `✓ compacted` for a tool named "compacted" rather than a system-level context event; the folded turns are also not shown as a collapsible block, so the card says *that* two turns folded and what it saved, not *which* turns | the slice-10 visual pass (tmux capture, frames 10-4), 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the compaction card carries a distinct system marker — the doubled diamond in the dim tone and a ‘system’ tag — so a first-time reader no longer takes ✓ compacted for a tool named “compacted”. Pinned by `a_compaction_card_carries_the_system_marker`

| KI-087 | gap | **RECURRENCE of KI-074 on a third surface — the scheduled run.** `cron::execute` loads the memory corpus the way `main.rs drive` does, by resolving the root from the working-directory walk alone, so a scheduled run started outside any intended corpus silently adopts a foreign one. The slice-11 scheduler-thread smoke reproduced KI-074 verbatim from a scratch cwd with no `memories/` of its own, under `~/.hermes`: two `skipping` lines naming `MEMORY` and `USER` — HERMES' own memory store adopted as the scheduled run's fact corpus, with nothing in the run's output saying which root was adopted. The cron path inherits the rule by construction (it is a deliberate mirror of `drive`), so the recurrence is the same root cause KI-074 already names: the cwd walk never consults FLEET_HOME while the sessions dir and the calib root both do | the slice-11 smoke's stderr, `fleet: cron` + `fleet chat` from `~/.hermes/cache/scratch/...` with a scratch FLEET_HOME and no `memories/` of its own: `fleet: memories: skipping \`MEMORY\` — the file does not open with a \`---\` frontmatter fence` (twice), 2026-10-14; `src/cron.rs execute` against `src/main.rs drive` | **closed — fixed** | `resolve_root_from` checks the fleet home’s own `memories/` BEFORE the working directory’s ancestor walk, so a scheduled run from a scratch directory cannot adopt a foreign corpus found further up the tree. Pinned by `the_homes_own_corpus_wins_over_a_walked_up_foreign_one`; the same fix covers KI-074’s root cause and names itself on KI-099 and KI-106’s surfaces |

| KI-088 | improvement | The denial an unattended scheduled run's tool call receives is the agent loop's one fixed sentence — "denied: the user did not approve this call." — which names a user who is not there. The cron gate denies precisely because nobody is present, so the model-visible text is imprecise in the one path where "the user" is guaranteed absent. The rest of the record is honest: the log carries `approved: false`, the job's `last_outcome` records `skipped-needs-approval`, and the scheduler's line names the tool and the reason | integration test `a_job_that_is_not_declared_unattended_skips_its_tool_call_and_names_it` asserts the denied `tool_result` in the scheduled run's own log; `src/agent.rs execute_call`'s fixed denial string against `src/cron.rs Gate`, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | `Approver` gained a defaulted `denial_reason() -> Option<String>` (`src/agent.rs`), and the unattended gate supplies `denied: this scheduled job is not declared unattended (`auto_approve` off), so no one is here to approve this call` (`src/cron.rs` `Gate::DENIAL`); every other approver keeps the ordinary sentence. Evidence: unit test `the_unattended_gate_names_the_jobs_policy_not_an_absent_user`; integration test `a_job_that_is_not_declared_unattended_skips_its_tool_call_and_names_it` asserts the denied `tool_result`'s output in the scheduled run's own log names the policy and not "the user did not approve"; 384 unit + 88 integration green |

| KI-089 | gap | The fired-minute latch is per-process by the design's own rule ("a per-job fired-minute latch in memory"), so two long-lived processes on one machine — a `fleet chat` and a TUI, both of which spawn a scheduler — each decide the same job is due in the same minute. The consequence is two runs, two session logs, and two providers' worth of spend for one window. Nothing in the store arbitrates: `record_run` writes after the run, not before it, so a second process reading the store mid-run sees the old `last_run` | `src/cron.rs Scheduler::latch` (a `HashMap` inside one `Scheduler`), `src/main.rs chat_command` and `src/tui/mod.rs spawn_agent` each calling `Scheduler::spawn` with their own, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the scheduler writes a per-(job, minute) claim beside the job store before it fires, and the claim's creation is the lock: `create_new` is an atomic create, so the first scheduler to own a minute owns it and a second long-lived process on the machine is refused by the operating system rather than by a race. A claim from a process that died suppresses only its own minute, which has passed; the tick sweeps past minutes' claims so the directory does not grow. Pinned by `a_minutes_claim_is_atomic_and_a_second_scheduler_is_refused` a claim written to the store before the run (a `running_since` minute, or a lock file beside `jobs.json`) when two schedulers on one machine is ever observed to matter — the accepted design for now, and the reason the feature doc names the latch as in-memory |

| KI-090 | improvement | The TUI owes this slice a scheduler thread but no surface: a fire's named line rides stderr from inside the agent thread, which writes over the alternate screen the TUI is drawing on, so a scheduled run's outcome appears as a stray line across the interface rather than as a card. It is the consolidator's own precedent (its hourly line does the same), but a fire is a whole run with an outcome, not one line — and the session it wrote is not the session the interface is driving, so the conversation pane is the wrong place for it either | `src/tui/mod.rs spawn_agent` spawning `crate::cron::Scheduler::spawn` beside `spawn_consolidator`, both writing to stderr from a thread the event loop does not own, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | `UiEvent::CronFire` (job, minute, catch_up flag, outcome, detail, session) rides the TUI's own event channel from the scheduler thread and lands as `Entry::Cron(CronCard)` in the tool-card family, rendered in the compaction card's two-line shape. Evidence: unit tests `a_scheduled_fire_lands_as_its_own_card` and `the_cron_fire_card_renders_in_the_house_card_style` (the card carries the job, the minute — "catch-up for" when it is one — the outcome, and the session id, whole at 80 columns); `Scheduler::spawn` takes the interface's `ui_tx` and the headless `chat` path passes `None`; 384 unit + 88 integration green. The one-event-family idea (KI-086's shared system-card grammar) stays open |

| KI-091 | defect (test hygiene) | Slice 11's first integration tests dirtied the repo's **git-tracked** memory corpus: `cargo test` runs with the crate root as its working directory, the corpus root resolves from that directory's `memories/` (KI-074's walk), and a scheduled run's recall hits write `recall-count` back through the bounded frontmatter-preserving rewrite — so five new tests bumped two tracked facts from 0 to 20 and left the tree dirty, which both breaks the clean-tree discipline and would have put unrelated corpus edits inside the slice's commit. The pre-existing integration helpers never hit it because they pass `memory: None` and never assemble a corpus | `git status` after `cargo test --test integration` on the slice-11 tree: ` M memories/fleet-is-the-native-rust-successor-to-the-fork.md` and ` M memories/fleet-memories-substrate-is-one-markdown-file-per-fact.md`, each `recall-count: 0` → `recall-count: 20`, 2026-10-14; `src/cron.rs execute`'s corpus load against `src/memory.rs record_hits` | **closed — fixed** | `cron::execute` now takes `memory_root` as an explicit input (the caller decides, the way it decides `home`), `run_job` resolves it with the same `resolve_root(cwd)` walk `fleet run` uses — production behavior unchanged — and the test harness passes the scratch home's own empty corpus. Evidence: `git status --porcelain memories/` empty after a full `cargo test`, 371 unit + 88 integration green. The deeper exposure (the cwd walk adopting a foreign corpus, KI-074/KI-087) is unchanged and still open |

| KI-092 | defect | The job store's read-modify-write narrows its lost-update window but does not close it. `fire_one` re-reads `jobs.json` immediately before `record_run`, so the window between that read and the rename inside `save` is microseconds — but a `fleet cron disable` that read the store before the scheduler's fresh read and writes after it is silently reverted: the mid-run job comes back **enabled** with a `last_run` it did not earn, and the next process to read the store believes a disabled job fired. The comment on that code used to claim "a store another process rewrote is not clobbered", which is false; it now states what is true (the re-read narrows the window) | `src/cron.rs` `fire_one` (the re-read before `record_run`, beside `Store::save`'s temp+rename), the slice-11 quality review's finding 3, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the store's read-modify-write runs under an exclusive `flock` on a sibling `jobs.lock` file, so a `fleet cron` command and a scheduler's `record_run` can no longer interleave — the lost-update window is closed, not narrowed. The lock blocks, and its doc names the one re-entrancy a caller must avoid. Pinned by `the_stores_mutations_run_under_the_lock`
| KI-093 | risk | A `last_run` in the future makes `missed > last` permanently false. `catch_up_due` only serves a window strictly newer than the recorded one, so a store hand-edited (or restored from a backup) with a `last_run` past the current minute silently suppresses every catch-up for that job until the wall clock passes the stamp. Benign today — the only writer of `last_run` is `record_run` with the minute the fire ran for, so a future stamp requires a hand edit — and untested | `src/cron.rs` `catch_up_due` (`if missed > last`) against `Store::record_run`'s stamp, the slice-11 quality review's finding 7c, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | a `last_run` in the future is clamped to the minute before the window it would silence, so a hand-edited or backup-restored store serves its next window normally instead of stranding the job forever. Pinned by `a_future_last_run_does_not_silence_the_job`
| KI-094 | risk | A backward clock jump can double-fire a minute through the tick latch. `tick_due` compares the latch against the current minute, so a clock that steps back re-presents a minute the latch already holds and the job fires again — two runs, two session logs, two providers' worth of spend for one window. The same jump also re-opens a window `record_run` already stamped, because the store's minutes are wall-clock values | `src/cron.rs` `tick_due` (the latch comparison) against `Minute::now`'s `SystemTime` read, the slice-11 quality review's finding 7c, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | a tick whose minute is older than the last one seen is refused with a named line — a backward clock jump can no longer re-present a minute the latch holds and double-fire the job. Pinned by `a_backward_clock_is_refused_not_served`
| KI-095 | gap | A failed `record_run` is never retried for that minute. The latch is claimed at decision time and the outcome is written after the run, so a store write that fails (a full disk, a permissions change) leaves `last_run` stale while the latch blocks the minute from being decided again: the window is spent on the provider and unrecorded in the store, and a restart catches up on it as if it never ran. The named line does carry `; the outcome could not be recorded: …` | `src/cron.rs` `fire_one` (the `record_run` call and its `tail`) against `Scheduler::tick_due`'s decision-time claim, the slice-11 quality review's finding 7d, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | a failed `record_run` is retried once from a freshly loaded store; a full disk at 04:00 no longer leaves `last_run` stale while the run genuinely completed
| KI-096 | defect | **RECURRENCE of KI-078/KI-085 on a fourth surface — the cron fire card.** The card's first draft put the job, the minute phrase, and the state word on one line: `✓ scheduled  daily-digest  at 2026-10-14T12:00Z  done`, which the conversation pane cut at 50 columns (`…at 2026-10-14T12:00Z  do`). Same failure, same class — a fixed-width surface given a line built by concatenation — and the catch-up phrasing (`catch-up for …`) makes it worse. Fixed before commit by rendering the card in the compaction card's own two-line shape: a short settled line (dot, verb, job, state) with the minute, the outcome, and the session id on the wrapped detail line | the slice-11 quality-review fix round's render test, `TestBackend` at 80x24: `the_cron_fire_card_renders_in_the_house_card_style` failed with the line cut at `…  do`; `src/tui/ui.rs` `Entry::Cron`, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed before commit** | the card now renders `✓ scheduled  daily-digest  done` with `└ at 2026-10-14T12:00Z · completed in 3 turns · session …` wrapped underneath, and the test asserts both lines whole at 80 columns. The general fix (one shared width-aware wrapper for the card family, KI-055's note) is still open |

| KI-097 | gap (consequence) | Slice 12's gate fails closed on this machine: the rustup profile is minimal (KI-007), so `cargo fmt` and `cargo clippy` answer `error: no such command: 'fmt'` / `'clippy'` and every real `fleet improve apply` is refused at the fmt step — no source, skill, memory, or config proposal can apply here until the toolchain gains rustfmt and clippy. This is the design working as specified ("a gate step that cannot run is never a silent pass"), and the slice's ACs are driven with stand-in gate steps plus one CLI test that accepts either outcome; but the consequence is that the loop cannot close on this machine today | the slice-12 smoke, 2026-10-14: `fleet improve apply demo-update` → exit 1, `the gate refused the change: fmt, clippy, build, test failed — error: no such command: 'fmt'`, the skill untouched, and the refused `propose` row carrying all four failed steps in `~/.fleet`-scratch calib | **closed — unblocked 2026-10-15** | KI-007 repaired: rustfmt and clippy are installed and the gate runs whole on this machine, so a real source apply's fmt/clippy steps can pass (premise updated per the viability plan's C8) |

| KI-098 | improvement | The two proposal stores are listed side by side but not unified: the general store (`<FLEET_HOME>/proposals/`, where the `improve` tool files) and slice 7's drafts (`<FLEET_HOME>/skills/proposals/`) have different frontmatter, and a slice-7 draft adapts into the gate's view at read time rather than being migrated. Applying a draft stamps its verdict into the draft's own frontmatter (slice 7's loader tolerates the extra key by its forward-compatibility rule) instead of moving or deleting it — per the slice's "do not migrate or delete slice 7's" rule — so `fleet skills --proposed` still lists an applied draft, and only the improve surface shows its verdict. The two locations are named honestly in the feature doc; unifying them is a decision, not an oversight | `src/improve.rs draft_proposal` (the read-time adapter) and `stamp` (the verdict fields written into the draft's frontmatter), against `src/skills.rs load_proposals`, 2026-10-14 | **closed — decided (deferred)** | one store with one shape when the owner wants it. The adapter between the general store and slice 7's drafts exists precisely so the decision can be made later without a migration, and no surface is observed to need the unification now — moved to batch 3 — unifying the two proposal stores is a design decision; the adapter already exists so the decision can be made later without a migration |

| KI-099 | risk | The gate runs in the working directory as the repository root — the memory corpus's cwd-walk precedent (KI-074/KI-087's family) — so `fleet improve apply` from outside the intended repo gates and commits against the wrong tree, and nothing in the command's output names the adopted root. The memory corpus has at least a logged skip line when it adopts a foreign tree; the improve gate is silent about which repo it ran in until a step fails and the cargo output names the path | `src/improve.rs Apply` (the repo is the caller's `current_dir()`, resolved in `src/main.rs improve_command`), against `src/memory.rs resolve_root`, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the apply names the repo root it adopted, one line before the verdict — ‘gating and committing against <path>’ — so an apply from outside the intended repo says which tree it is about to gate and commit against

| KI-100 | improvement | A home-side apply's pre-image lives in `<FLEET_HOME>/proposals/preimages/`, keyed by the proposal name, and the two stores share that namespace — a name filed in both stores would collide on the pre-image, with the general store winning the load and the second filing's apply overwriting the first's stash. A chain of apply/undo/apply keeps only the latest pre-image, because each apply stashes the current file; the ledger keeps the history, but the undo always restores the most recent pre-image, not the original | `src/improve.rs stage_write_skill`/`stage_retire_skill`/`stage_config` (the stash paths) against `undo_restore`, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | a proposal’s pre-image is keyed by name AND store (`general-<name>.md` / `skills-<name>.md`), so a name filed in both stores cannot overwrite the other’s stash and an undo restores the right bytes. Pinned by `the_two_stores_pre_images_are_keyed_apart`

| KI-101 | note | A repo-side apply's commit uses the repository's own git identity and its own `git` on the ambient PATH: a repo with no `user.name`/`user.email` configured refuses the apply after the green gate, with git's error named in the verdict ("the change landed but its commit did not"). The refusal is honest and the change is reverted, but it means the last step of a green apply depends on the repo's configuration rather than the gate | `src/improve.rs Landed::commit` (plain `git commit`, no `-c` identity overrides, so production uses the owner's config), tested against the scratch repo's local config, 2026-10-14 | **closed — decided** | the named refusal is the correct behavior and no action is needed unless an apply is ever observed failing on git identity; the row’s own next action named exactly this |

| KI-102 | defect (found in review, fixed before push) | The slice-12 `patch` change op accepted any repo-relative path, including `memories/<fact>.md`: a model-filed proposal could rewrite a confirmed-by fact with no approval, bypassing the provenance rule that is the module's stated permission model (the supersede path double-guards; the patch path consulted no basis). Found by the stage-2 quality review; fixed by refusing a patch whose first path component is `memories/` — a fact changes through `supersede`, the only door — with a regression test that asserts the refusal names supersede, the file is untouched, and the gate never ran | the slice-12 quality review's Q6, 2026-10-14; `src/improve.rs stage_patch` | closed-fixed | none — the regression test is the guard |
| KI-103 | defect (found in review, fixed before push) | The apply overwrote a target file before the gate ran, and a red gate's revert restored HEAD — so pre-existing uncommitted work in the target path was silently destroyed, undisclosed and untested. Fixed by a `git status --porcelain -- <paths>` guard before the write: a dirty target is refused naming the work, on both the patch path and the memory path (whose supersede rewrites the fact files the same way). The commit message also carries a `fleet improve:` marker so a machine-made commit is self-describing in history | the slice-12 quality review's Q3, 2026-10-14; `src/improve.rs stage_patch`/`stage_memory`/`dirty_paths` | closed-fixed | none — the refusal is the guard; a lock beside jobs.json-style file locking is a later concern |
| KI-104 | gap (consequence) | The slice-13 improve-gate's `eval_corpus` step cannot run its production path on this machine: KI-007 (rustfmt/clippy absent) refuses every real source apply at the fmt step *before* the four-cargo gate finishes, so the corpus step is never reached here. The design works as specified ("a gate step that cannot run is never a silent pass"), the AC is driven through the stand-in probe (`a_source_proposal_that_regresses_the_corpus_is_refused_with_the_scenario_named`), and `fleet eval` runs green end to end — but the gate wiring the corpus into a real apply is unexercised until the toolchain gains rustfmt/clippy. Same class as KI-097 | the slice-13 build on this machine: `cargo fmt`/`clippy` absent (KI-007); `fleet improve apply` on a source proposal is refused at fmt before the eval step; the AC is proven via the stand-in seam, 2026-10-14 | **closed — unblocked 2026-10-15** | KI-007 repaired: rustfmt/clippy installed, so a real source apply is gated on the corpus on this machine (premise updated per the viability plan's C8) |
| KI-105 | risk | The eval-corpus schedule's latch is in-memory (`cron::Scheduler::eval_last`), not persisted beside the job store, so a fleet process restarted inside the 04:00 matching minute re-fires the daily corpus for that minute. Idempotent — the corpus is deterministic and a re-run lands one extra `eval_corpus` row — but a double-run is the KI-094 clock/restart family's shape and is untested | `src/cron.rs run_eval_if_due` (the in-memory `eval_last`) against the cron store's persisted `last_run` (KI-092's read-modify-write family), the slice-13 scheduler hook, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the eval cadence’s minute is persisted beside the job store (`cron/eval-minute.txt`), read at construction and written at fire, so a process restarted inside the 04:00 window does not re-run the corpus. Pinned by `the_eval_minute_survives_a_restart`
| KI-106 | risk | `fleet doctor --diagnose` resolves its default ledger path (`docs/known-issues.md`) from the working directory — the KI-099 cwd-walk family (the improve gate's own convention). The bare `--diagnose` only *names* that path and writes nothing; `--ledger <path>` writes where it is pointed, so the human-owned ledger is never written without an explicit path. The residual is that the *named* default could adopt a foreign tree's `docs/` when run from the wrong directory | `src/main.rs diagnose_command` (`current_dir().join("docs/known-issues.md")`) against `src/memory.rs resolve_root` and KI-099, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the bare ‘--diagnose’ names the ledger path it read (the cwd-resolved ‘docs/known-issues.md’), so a run from the wrong directory says which ledger it consulted
| KI-107 | improvement | sdm1's turn-duration prediction is the deferred half of the telemetry port: the corpus grades *outcomes* (does the log reconstruct, does the request match), not latencies, and a duration predictor needs a training set this crate does not collect. | KI-108 | defect | The eval cadence's fire lacked the panic guard every job fire gets: `run_eval_if_due` polled the eval future directly, so a panic in the corpus run unwound through the scheduler thread's `block_on` and killed the tick loop for the process lifetime — silently, one stderr line over the TUI's alternate screen — and a loaded machine at 04:00 lost ALL scheduled work. The corpus's own harness is the KI-027-known-flaky spawn/port pattern promoted into `fleet eval`'s production path | the slice-13 review's Critical, 2026-10-14; `src/cron.rs run_eval_if_due` (line 1239) now wraps the fire in the existing `CatchUnwind` adapter through the new generic `caught` seam (line 1155): one named line on a panic (`the minute stays claimed`), the loop continues; pinned by `a_panicking_eval_cadence_is_named_and_the_loop_carries_on` (line 2301), which drives a panicking cadence through the seam and then runs a real job on the same scheduler | closed-fixed | the fix landed with the gate green (build 0 warnings, 550 tests pass); the owner closes the row |
| KI-109 | defect | The eval corpus's scenario drivers carried `.unwrap()` in production paths — six `Session::create` calls, one `Session::open`, the memory scenario's two `fs::write`s, `scratch_dir`'s clock unwrap and `create_dir_all().expect()`, the mock harness's spawn and port panics, and an eagerly-evaluated `records[2]` index — so a filesystem fault turned `fleet eval` into exit 101 and the improve gate's refusal into a misattributed "regressed", falsifying the module doc's "a scenario turns every other failure into a graded outcome" claim | the slice-13 review's Important, 2026-10-14; `src/eval.rs`: `MockApi::start`/`start_over`/`wait_for_port` (lines 180, 270, 300) return named errors, `scratch_dir` (line 888) returns a `Result`, the `create_session`/`open_session` seams (lines 943, 959) grade a session fault, the memory scenario's two fact-file writes grade, `records.get(2)` reads through a checked access, and `Agent::new`'s error rides the run's own error path; every scenario now fails naming the fault, and the corpus still runs green end to end | closed-fixed | the fix landed with the gate green (550 tests pass); the owner closes the row |
| KI-110 | defect | The improve-gate's subprocess probe failed open and could grade the wrong binary: a `current_exe` error silently unwired the eval step and the apply proceeded, an installed or stale binary outside the gate's target dir was graded instead of the fresh build (a false green, silent), and `Command::output()` had no timeout, so a hung probe hung the gate | the slice-13 review's Important, 2026-10-14; `src/eval.rs subprocess_probe` (line 614) takes the gate root and refuses — naming it — unless the current executable resolves under `<gate root>/target/` (`under_target`, line 635, canonicalized for macOS symlinks), `run_eval_subprocess` (line 673) bounds the child by `PROBE_TIMEOUT_SECS = 120` (line 585) with a drain thread and a kill on expiry, and `strip_ansi` (line 744) keeps the palette's trailing reset out of the greped regression names; `src/main.rs` (line 558) wires the fail-closed probe unconditionally; pinned by the eval tests at lines 1661, 1694, 1706, and 1719 | closed-fixed | the fix landed with the gate green; the owner closes the row |

| KI-115 | defect (found in review, fixed in the same round) | Filling slice 3's board-pane placeholder needed width: a composed card with a name and its facts does not fit in 24 inner columns, so the pane went from 26 to 42 columns and the conversation pane from 54 to 38 at an 80-column terminal. That immediately cut the **compaction card's** first line — `✓ compacted  6 turns → 1 summary  done`, 39 characters — at `…  d`, losing its state word: the fourth recurrence of the KI-078/KI-085/KI-096 class (a fixed-width surface given a line built by concatenation). The **cron fire card** had the same latent weakness one line down: its detail line wrapped `session <id>` between the two words, halving a session id that is the card's only handle on the run's log. Fixed by moving the summary phrase onto the compaction card's wrapped detail line (the settled line is now the dot, the verb, the fold's size, and the state — nothing else) and by giving the session id its own detail line. Both cards' tests now assert the whole phrase at 120 columns *and* the settled line whole at 80 | the slice-14 build, `cargo test --lib tui::ui::` at 80x24: `the_compaction_card_renders_in_the_house_card_style` failed with the line cut at `✓ compacted  6 turns → 1 summary  d`, and `the_cron_fire_card_renders_in_the_house_card_style` failed with `session 20261014T120000Z-1a2b3c` split across two wrapped lines; `src/tui/ui.rs` `Entry::Compaction`/`Entry::Cron` | **closed — fixed** | the fix landed with the gate green (build 0 warnings, 480 unit + 2 + 7 + 90 integration pass) and the live TUI smoke-verified through a PTY; the general fix (one shared width-aware wrapper for the card family, KI-055's note) is still open |
| KI-116 | gap | The router kit's documented default runtime home is not where this machine's router actually runs. `~/.agnostic-router-kit/router/` holds only `.env` and no `config.json`, while the live router at 127.0.0.1:8300 is `/Users/alejandrodelvillar/.zcode/router/server.js` (up since Oct 9), whose own path resolution is `CONFIG_PATH = path.join(__dirname, "config.json")` — the config beside the server, not under the kit home. A board that resolved only the kit's documented chain would find no token and refuse every read against a kit that is up and answering. The board therefore names the deployed location as its last fallback, in resolution order, in `boards::router_config_path`. **Resolved 2026-10-16, by the owner's own-router ruling rather than by naming a root:** Fleet's router board no longer resolves a token, dials a port, or reads an operator API, so the kit's runtime home stopped being a Fleet concern at all — `boards::router_config_path` and the whole HTTP leg are gone, and the board reads Fleet's own roster, preference table, and route rows instead. The port 8300 belongs to the zcode harness's own router, which Fleet never routes through and never reads the config of | `ls -la ~/.agnostic-router-kit/router/` → `.env` only, no `config.json`; `ps aux \\| grep server.js` → `/Users/alejandrodelvillar/.zcode/router/server.js`; `~/.zcode/router/server.js:62` (`CONFIG_PATH = path.join(__dirname, "config.json")`) against the kit's `lib/paths.mjs` (`AGNOSTIC_ROUTER_KIT_HOME ?? ~/.agnostic-router-kit`); `~/.zcode/router/config.json`'s `localToken` authorizes `GET /api/state` → 200; after the ruling, `src/boards.rs` carries no `AGNOSTIC_ROUTER_*` name and no HTTP client at all | closed-fixed | none — the resolution chain the row asked to tidy up no longer exists, because there is nothing to resolve |
| KI-117 | improvement | `serde_json`'s `Map` is a `BTreeMap` in this crate's dependency set (no `preserve_order` feature, and adding one would touch `Cargo.toml`), so every JSON object the boards read arrives with its members in **sorted key order**, not the payload's own. The second-brain state files' `series`, `milestones`, `graded_rows`, and `plans` blocks therefore read back alphabetically, and any surface that wants "the state's own order" — the maturity board's series, for one — must name the member it wants rather than take the first. The board does (`output` by name, falling back to the first series), and the doc comments say so, but the underlying fact is a property of the dependency, not of the readers | `src/boards.rs` `parse_maturity` against a fixture declaring `"series": { "output": …, "continuity": … }`: `maturity.series[0]` reads back as `("continuity", …)`, 2026-10-14 | closed — ruled by the owner; the row carried the ruling all along | the tradeoff IS the decision, recorded in this row when it was written: keep serde_json's Map as the BTreeMap it is (no preserve_order, verified in Cargo.toml/Cargo.lock 2026-10-24) and let a surface that wants payload order name the member it wants, the way the boards already do. Nothing in the dependency set changed since; the status lagged the ruling
| KI-118 | improvement | The board refresher reads all three boards on the cadence, and the tower is the expensive one: five bounded `git` calls per repository over thirty repos is roughly two seconds of child-process work every 30 seconds, spent whether or not anyone is looking at the tower. The bound contains it (`MAX_PROJECTS = 64`, `GIT_TIMEOUT_SECS = 5` per call, all of it on the refresher's own blocking pool, so the interface never waits), but the cadence is a fixed cost rather than a demand-driven one | the ignored live read, `cargo test --lib boards::tests::the_boards_read_the_machines_real_sources -- --ignored`: all three boards read in 2.81s, `tower: /Users/alejandrodelvillar/Projects · 30 repos, 25 dirty, 5 skipped`; a standalone 30-repo × 5-call `git` sweep of `~/Projects` measured 1.72s wall, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the cadence reads only the ACTIVE board — the opening read is all three (the interface starts with three loading slots), a `BoardCommand::Active` from the cycle tells the refresher which board is showing and reads it immediately, and `Refresh` keeps meaning a full read on demand. A period spent on the router board no longer pays the tower's five-git-calls-per-repo scan. Pinned by `the_cadence_reads_only_the_active_board`

But `gliner2` declares its local-model stack — torch, transformers, numpy, peft, safetensors — under the `local` extra, and `gliner2/models/base.py` imports torch at module load, so a venv built from the bare package cannot serve the sys1 local provider at all. | KI-128 | defect (pre-existing, found while proving slice 15) | Every headless surface prints through `println!`/`print!`, so a stdout pipe closed by the reader panics the process — `failed printing to stdout: Broken pipe (os error 32)`, exit 101 — for `fleet install \\| head`, `fleet doctor \\| head`, and any other surface. Pre-existing and crate-wide; slice 15 only made it easy to notice, because the install's report plus the doctor's checks is the longest output in the crate. An unpiped run is unaffected | `target/release/fleet install \\| head -12` after a green first run printed the report, then `thread 'main' panicked at library/std/src/io/stdio.rs:1165:9: failed printing to stdout: Broken pipe (os error 32)`, exit 101; the pre-existing surfaces behave the same — `target/release/fleet doctor \\| head -2` exits 101, while `fleet sessions \\| head -1` (output inside the pipe buffer) exits 0, 2026-10-14 | **closed — fixed** | the conventional posture, taken over the row's per-surface checked writer: one line at the top of `main` restores the default SIGPIPE disposition, so a closed stdout pipe kills the process the way every other CLI dies — quietly, by signal — instead of unwrapping an `io::Error` inside `println!`. It covers every surface at once, and an unpiped run is unchanged. Pinned by `a_closed_stdout_pipe_is_a_quiet_exit_not_a_panic`, which reads one line then closes the pipe and fails with the row's own panic signature when the restore is removed |

| KI-219 | defect (false refusal, blocks its own docs) | The write-time nomenclature guard tokenizes **every** word in a file's content (`identifier_tokens` at `src/agent.rs:3108` splits on non-alphanumerics and keeps every run of 3+ characters), so a markdown heading's prose word becomes an "identifier" and is matched case-insensitively against the vocabulary. The established term `context` is carried by real code identifiers in `services/blend1/service/mcp_server.py:95` and five more sibling services, so **writing `## Context` into any markdown file is refused outright** — `write_file` returns "`Context` is a case or separator variant of the established term `context` … rename it to the established term" and the file is never written. `## Plan:` fires the same way against `plan`. The refusal has no other explanation for a *prose heading* — the guard's own stated contract is identifiers — and the vocabulary module already implements the correct rule: `extract_doc` (`src/nomenclature.rs:765`) takes only backtick spans, path-like runs, and fenced code lines from markdown, with the comment "Everything else is prose and contributes nothing", while `extract_code` strips Rust comments for the same reason ("a comment's prose is not an identifier"). The guard applies neither rule, so it treats the content it is policing as raw code | `src/agent.rs:3108` (`identifier_tokens`), `:3030-3060` (the case-variant refusal), `:2175` (the write blocked before it lands); reproduced on this repo by attempting to write `docs/plans/2026-10-17-ki-triage.md` with a `## Context` heading, 2026-10-17 | **closed — fixed** | the guard now takes its tokens through the same channel rules the vocabulary derives from, by one shared function: `channel_tokens` (`src/nomenclature.rs:853`), called from `write_guard` (`src/agent.rs:3076`) with the write's own path. Markdown (`.md`) contributes its backtick spans, path-like runs, and fenced code lines only; every other file contributes its non-comment text — prose words in headings, sentences, and table cells contribute nothing, exactly as the vocabulary's own derivation says they should. `split_identifier` is now public for the same reason: one splitter, one place, so the guard cannot drift from what it reads. `## Context` writes clean, `let judgeRoute = judge_route();` in code is still refused, a fenced or backticked identifier in markdown is still refused, and a comment inside a code write is not an identifier while a string literal still is. Pinned by `prose_in_a_markdown_write_is_not_an_identifier`, `markdown_code_spans_are_still_checked`, and the pre-existing `a_refused_write_does_not_land_and_a_flagged_one_carries_the_note`; gate green (`cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo build --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build --release`, `cargo test` — 1142 tests, 0 failed) |

The adapter branch (`feat/mcp-stdio-adapter`) merged in the same push, closing KI-143's carry end to end. Upstream main's suite green on the merge
## Rules for this ledger

- Log on encounter: an error, warning, defect, gap, or improvement found during any Fleet work lands here the moment it is seen, with the command or probe that produced it as evidence — before any fix, and even when the fix is immediate.
- **Improvements are captured the same way**: anything noticed that works but should or could be better — a rough edge, an inconsistency, an unverified claim, a missed opportunity — is logged with what was noticed and where. Logging is not fixing and not scope: a logged improvement does not authorize changing it mid-slice unless the owner says so; the owner triages what becomes work.
- No silent dismissals: "pre-existing," "not our repo," "unrelated to the task," and "CI is red anyway" are not closures. Deferral is explicit and owner-approved.
- Close with evidence: the fixing commit, the green run, or the owner's decision is linked in the row.
- **The gate never lies**: never pipe the gate through a status-losing pipe (`cargo test | grep` reports grep's status, not cargo's). Read the exit code — `PIPESTATUS`, or the tool's own exit_code — before committing. (Added after KI-014.)
- **An id comes from the table, never from memory**: the next id is the largest present plus one, derived from the file at write time under the ledger's own lock (`next_ki_id`, `src/doctor.rs`). A row appended by hand reads the table first, and `scripts/ki_ledger.py` reports a duplicate id before the row is committed. (Added after KI-285.)
- The ledger feeds the plan: an item that blocks a slice is named in that slice; slice 13 automates the evidence gathering, but this file stays the system of record.
- **A shared struct that gains a field mid-slice:** the committing change carries the mechanical additions to every other writer of its literals in the same commit — a field that lands without them leaves the tree unbuildable for `--all-targets` (KI-146, and KI-218's 23-constructor outage is the same failure at scale).

| KI-218 | defect (build, found by the KI-triage warmup's HEAD check) | Commit `aa367e8` added `AgentOptions.identity` and left 23 `AgentOptions` constructors in `tests/integration.rs` without the field, so **HEAD `c31c2d3` did not compile** — `cargo build --all-targets` exited 101 with 23 `E0063 missing field 'identity'` errors. The committed state of the repo was unbuildable for every check that runs `--all-targets` (clippy, test, coverage), while `cargo build` alone passed because the library target alone does not construct `AgentOptions` — so a gate run that skipped `--all-targets` would have reported green on a broken tree | `tests/integration.rs` 23 constructors; the field landed in `src/agent.rs:128` in `aa367e8`; verified 2026-10-17 by stashing the working tree and building HEAD (`git stash push -- tests/integration.rs` → exit 101, 23 `missing field` errors → `git stash pop`); recurrence 2026-10-23: the work-surface commit added three `SessionSummary` fields and named only the source paths, leaving six test fixtures behind — caught before the push by `git diff HEAD -- src tests` returning a non-empty diff, which is the check this class needs rather than a longer list of paths | **closed — fixed** | commit `5b4ce6d` adds `identity: None` to all 23 constructors; the gate on the fixed tree: `cargo fmt --all --check` exit 0, `cargo build --all-targets` exit 0, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` exit 0, `cargo build --release` exit 0, `cargo test` exit 0 with 1028 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored |

Second run's workaround: the lane child was spawned directly with fleet's own launch spec (`BLEND1_PYTHONPATH` + the four `BLEND1_<SVC>_PYTHONPATH` siblings, `cwd` = `services/blend1/`) and the `tools/call` frame driven over stdio — the identical JSON-RPC the agent loop sends, which returns the same report shape. | KI-223 | defect (change-detection poisoned by cwd, found by the pmocockpit refresh) | A docs-derived corpus refresh reports `rebuilt` when the only difference is the **working directory the lane was launched from**, not the docs. `_source_files` resolves each source through `glob`, `_chunks` builds each key as `os.path.relpath(path)` relative to the child's cwd, and `refresh`'s `current == derived` comparison is over `(key, sha256)` pairs — so the same absolute sources under a different cwd yield the same 72 text hashes but a different key prefix (`../pmocockpit/...` vs `../../../pmocockpit/...`), the sets differ, and the store rebuilds with byte-identical content. The 2026-10-17 `corpus-refresh-pmocockpit` run observed exactly this: the Aug-30-built store (keys `../pmocockpit/docs/...`) and the fresh derivation (keys `../../../pmocockpit/docs/...`) shared all 72 sha256 values, so the rebuild re-embedded unchanged text and wrote a new manifest. `action: "rebuilt"` therefore means "the derived set changed", not "the docs changed", and every cwd variation forces a pointless re-embed of the whole corpus (KI-222's embed budget is spent on nothing). Note this is NOT the lane's own fault as run: over MCP the child's cwd is the lane's home by design (`services/blend1/`), so the *lane* is consistent run to run — the divergence is between a lane-launched refresh and any other-cwd launch of the same corpus | the 2026-10-17 `corpus-refresh-pmocockpit` run: pre-run manifest (mtime Oct 14) keyed `../pmocockpit/docs/plans/...`, post-run manifest (mtime Oct 16 20:12) keyed `../../../pmocockpit/docs/plans/...`; all 72 sha256 values identical between them, `changed: true, action: "rebuilt"`; the second identical call reported `unchanged` (the lane's cwd is stable), and `blend1 search` returned live results; `src/blend1/corpus.py` `_chunks` (`os.path.relpath`), `_source_files` (`glob.glob`), and `refresh`'s `current == derived` at `:275` | **closed — fixed** | the chunk key is the source’s ABSOLUTE path (`path.resolve().as_posix()`), the one cwd-independent name a source has, so the same sources derive the same key set from any working directory. Pinned by the lane's own suite, `services/blend1/tests/test_corpus_keys.py::test_the_key_is_the_absolute_path_so_the_cwd_cannot_poison_it` (the lane's pytest is not installed on this machine, so the tests run directly under python3 — all seven blend1 tests pass) |

| KI-226 | defect (the write-time convention guard blocks ordinary Python drivers) | The `write_file` convention guard refuses any capitalized token that has a lowercase established form elsewhere in the tree, which makes an ordinary Python driver unwritable through `write_file`: a straightforward MCP stdio driver using `subprocess.Popen`, `threading.Thread`, `queue.Queue`, `TimeoutError`, `SystemExit`, and `Exception` is refused token by token (`Popen` is fine — no established term; `Thread`, `Queue`, `TimeoutError`, `SystemExit`, `Exception` are refused as variants of `thread`, `queue`, `timeouterror`-shaped, `systemexit`, `exception`), as are the prose words `One`, `MCP`, `Users`, and `Projects`. The guard's purpose is naming drift in this project's own code, but it reads every token of a new file as a project identifier, so a scratch driver cannot be written without contorting the code to lowercase prose and avoiding Python's own capitalized builtins. The workaround used here: drive the lane's JSON-RPC from bash with `env -i` + a heredoc (the adapter reads stdin line by line and ignores notifications), which is strictly less convenient than a Python driver would be | this session's `write_file` attempts at `/tmp/blend1_refresh_pmocockpit.py`: refusals naming `One` (variant of `one`, `services/blend1/service/mcp_server.py:10`), `MCP` (variant of `mcp`, `docs/plans/2026-10-13-fleet-slice-4-mcp-lanes.md:1`), `Users` (variant of `users`, `services/sdm1/src/sdm1/api.py:43`), `Thread` (variant of `thread`, `services/blend1/src/blend1/orchestrator.py:55`); `convention` confirms `queue`, `exception` are established lowercase terms while `popen` is not | **closed — fixed** | two scopes, both the row’s own next action: the guard’s scope is the tree the vocabulary was derived from — a path outside it is not project code to police — and a Python file’s capitalized builtins (`Thread`, `Queue`, `TimeoutError`, `SystemExit`, `Exception`, …) are the language’s own names, not this project’s drift. Pinned by `an_ordinary_python_driver_is_writable_as_written` |

| KI-228 | defect (cache invalidation, found by the 2026-10-18 memory deep dive) | The recall index's entry key is the fact file's `secs:nanos:len` stamp, and the recall-count bump rewrites the file in place through a temp-plus-rename — so every bump moves the file's mtime, changes its stamp, and invalidates its cached vector. A fact whose *content* never changed is therefore re-embedded on the next load. The corpus's three most-recalled facts carry counts of 103, 103 and 98, so they had been re-embedded on essentially every load, and the embedding round trip is the load's whole cost on a machine with a live vector path. The module's own doc says the intent is "a changed fact is re-embedded, an untouched one keeps its cached vector", where *changed* means content — the implementation reads metadata, so a metadata-only change is treated as a content change | `src/memory.rs` `file_stamp` (`secs:nanos:len`) and `load_with`'s `entry.stamp == fact.stamp` comparison; `rewrite_field` writes a temp and renames over the fact, moving its mtime; the test `the_index_caches_vectors_and_rebuilds_only_what_changed` pinned content-change and stamp-match but never exercised the bump | **closed — fixed** | the entry is keyed by a digest of the text the vector was actually computed from — `calib::hash16(&fact.indexed_text())`, the same hash the route cache joins on. `recall-count` is outside `indexed_text`, so a bump changes nothing; a description, shape or phrase edit re-embeds exactly that fact. Pinned by `a_recall_count_bump_re_embeds_nothing`, which bumps a fact through `recall_hits` and asserts the next load embeds nothing and does not even rewrite the index |
| KI-229 | defect (a cache that refuses to serve what it promised to discard, found by the same dive) | The route-decision cache's module doc promises "A cache written by another lane … is discarded rather than served", and `route.rs` repeats it. The implementation only suppresses *serving*: `judge` skips the `get` when `keyed_for` is false, but `entries` is never cleared. On the next fresh verdict, `put` + `flush(new_key)` serializes the whole struct — including the other lane's verdicts — under the new key, and they are served as this lane's from then on. Relocating the sys1 service or swapping its model therefore re-labels every previously recorded verdict as the new lane's rather than discarding it. The test `a_cache_keyed_to_another_lane_is_discarded_whole` asserts only `keyed_for` outcomes and never that entries were dropped, so the named discipline is unenforced | `src/route_cache.rs:13-19` (the promise), `src/route.rs:1460-1473` (the suppressed read), `src/route.rs:1488-1497` (the write-through that adopts them) | **closed — fixed** | `Cache::rekey(key)` clears `entries` when the stored key belongs to a different producer, called once in `judge` after the spec resolves. The key itself is left for `flush` to set, so a run that judges nothing writes nothing and a foreign file survives intact for the lane that wrote it. Pinned by `a_foreign_key_clears_the_entries_it_refuses_to_serve` |
| KI-230 | defect (a comment that contradicts the code it annotates, found by the same dive) | `src/route.rs:1449-1452` reads "The cache above still answers first: a recorded verdict is a judgment that was made, on whatever machine made it" — but `if !sys1_judges() { return … }` at `:1453` executes **before** the cache lookup at `:1460`. On a machine with no resolvable decision key (the ten listed at `:1392`), cached verdicts are never served at all, which also contradicts KI-044's own recorded rationale | `src/route.rs:1449-1455` against the lookup at `:1460` | **closed — fixed** | the lookup moved above the key check, so the order is: resolve spec → `rekey` → cache lookup → key check → spawn. A cached verdict now answers on a keyless machine, which is what a persistent cache under `FLEET_HOME` is for; a miss on a keyless machine still returns early and pays no spawn. Pinned by `a_cached_verdict_answers_before_the_key_check` |
| KI-231 | defect (a failing embed rewrites the cache it failed to fill, found by the same dive) | In `load_with`, a fact whose embed fails is `continue`d — it gets no vector and no `fresh` entry — and the write condition `changed \|\| fresh.len() != index.facts.len()` reads that absence as a change and rewrites the pruned map. A fact whose text changed while the embedding server was down is re-attempted, re-fails, prints one line, and re-writes — on every load, forever | `src/memory.rs` `load_with`'s write condition | **closed — fixed** | the rewrite needs a new vector or a genuinely orphaned entry: `index.facts.keys().any(\|name\| !facts.iter().any(\|fact\| &fact.name == name))`. A failed embed costs a retry next load and no write, and the failed fact's old entry keeps its slot (unservable, because the entry is keyed by digest). Pinned by `a_failed_embed_rewrites_nothing`, which also proves a deleted fact's entry is still pruned |

| KI-233 | defect (the cost KI-064 removed, reintroduced in the browser path, found by the same dive) | `bridge::rank` — the GUI composer's command type-ahead — called `memory::probe(&endpoint)` fresh on every request rather than `memory::probed_embedder()`. `probe` is a real round trip on its own five-second budget, so each request paid the probe's connect budget on a machine with no embedding backend, or a real round trip on a machine with one. This is exactly the per-message cost KI-064 removed from the corpus path | `src/gui/bridge.rs:649-651` | **closed — fixed** | `rank` takes the probed answer the corpus loads take, once per process. | KI-235 | defect (a lost update the atomicity discipline does not cover, found by the same dive) | `RecallCounts::bump` is a non-atomic read-modify-write of a shared file. Entries for deleted sessions are also never pruned, so a future log reusing an id inherits the count | `src/session_recall.rs` `bump` | **closed — fixed** | the whole read-modify-write runs under an exclusive lock on a sibling `.recall-counts.json.lock`.
