| KI-242 | risk (a stale face a rebuild cannot force, found while diagnosing the owner's missing-rail report) | The GUI's assets are served with no cache validators at all — no `Cache-Control`, no `ETag`, no `Last-Modified` on `/`, `/app.js`, or `/app.css` — and the asset URLs carry neither the launch token nor a content version (`script src="/app.js"`). A browser that cached `app.js` under that token-less URL could show a face from an older binary after a rebuild — precisely the "my GUI is missing its features" report this investigation chased. Verified live: Chromium refetches a validator-less response rather than reusing it (old content served, content swapped underneath, reload fetched fresh), and Firefox's disk cache held no entry, so the exposure is theoretical today — one aggressive browser heuristic away from a repeat of the confusion | `src/gui/server.rs`'s asset replies (headers read by curl: none); the script tag in `src/gui/assets/index.html`; the Playwright swap test of 2026-10-19 | closed — shipped by the PWA install work as stamped-immutable assets, stronger than the no-store asked | asset replies carry validators: documents answer `Cache-Control: no-cache` and stamped assets answer `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` off the build stamp in the URL (`?v=`), so a rebuild changes the stamp and the browser refetches — pinned by `src/gui/server.rs` tests asserting both headers; the token-less asset URL stands as deliberate (the cookie authenticates)
| KI-243 | defect (the semantic type-ahead silently answering zeros, found by the live type-ahead audit 2026-10-19) | The composer's semantic pass intermittently ranked everything at zero while the embed endpoint was healthy. Mechanism: the embed server closes an idle keep-alive connection after five seconds (`Keep-Alive: timeout=5`, llama-server on 127.0.0.1:8901), and `HttpEmbedder::embed` rides ureq's pooled connection — one request after an idle gap fails in milliseconds on the dead socket, `None` turns the whole ranking into zeros, and the type-ahead's honest degradation quietly makes the pass vanish. The owner's intent query ranked lexically whenever they paused to think. Verified live: a rank after a 6.5-second idle returned zeros before the fix and a real score after it | `src/memory.rs` `HttpEmbedder::embed`; the endpoint's `Keep-Alive: timeout=5` header; the Playwright rank-replies capture (zero before, 0.808 after) | **closed — fixed** | one retry in `embed`: the failed attempt burns the dead pooled connection and the second rides a fresh one. A genuinely down backend pays one extra failed attempt and answers the same. The standing alternative — disabling keep-alive reuse — was rejected as a permanent latency cost for a race one retry absorbs |

The engine behind the pass was fine — the pass was only ever aimed at what the owner had already typed past | `src/gui/assets/app.js` `typeAhead`'s first-word guard against the timer in `openCommands`; the network capture (one rank request, `asked: "make the scr"`, nothing after) | **closed — fixed** | the debounce re-arms on every keystroke in `typeAhead` — each key resets the 180ms timer, so the rank fires 180ms after the owner stops typing and ranks the sentence as typed; the first-word guard keeps only the lexical repaint |

Tab and clicking a row run the highlighted command; Enter, the most habitual key of the three, is the one that misfires | `src/gui/assets/app.js`, the composer keydown's Enter block (the exact-match parse and the `send()` fall-through) | **closed — fixed** | when the type-ahead is open and holds candidates, Enter should run the highlighted one — the same thing Tab already does — leaving the exact-match parse and plain sends for the closed case. Twelve of the last fourteen scheduled runs ended `max-turns` (the other: one completed, one in flight) — the corpus refreshes have effectively never succeeded. The fix is one line of wiring, but it is an approval-design decision first: these jobs run unattended with `auto_approve: true`, and putting lane tools — which reach external services — into an unattended run changes what auto-approve means. Design before code: either the task's named tools ride a per-job allowlist, or lane calls in scheduled runs cross the same gate a human session's do | `src/cron.rs:1291` (`lane_specs: None`); the jobs in `<FLEET_HOME>/cron/jobs.json` whose tasks name lane tools | closed — the per-job allowlist, and the live store carries it | the recorded ruling (put to the owner, unanswered; built as the plan recommends): a job carries an optional `tools` allowlist — the seed writes `mcp__blend1__refresh` on every corpus-refresh job — and the cron runner resolves each named tool's server into the run's lane specs (vendored lanes first; the registry reserves their names), so the lane tool is callable and lazy-joined and nothing else joins. Pinned by `a_jobs_allowlist_resolves_its_server_and_nothing_else` (the named lane joins, no list means no lanes at all, an unknown name is skipped with the rest resolving). Nothing serializes those three steps, the missed windows are caught up by several runs at once, so two runs can hand-append the same id and the file holds two rows under it; the checker catches that after the fact rather than preventing it. The same concurrency has a write-side face: an append's anchor text can vanish between the read that produced it and the edit that consumes it, because a sibling run is appending to the same row | the checker's report while both rows existed — `rows: 330   open: 14` / `PROBLEMS (1): docs/known-issues.md:393 (KI-329): duplicate ID, first seen at line 392` — line 392 being the `lifecycle-sweep` job's row (its own header: "found by this run — the 2026-10-27T02:30Z catch-up, executed 2026-10-27T03:46Z") and line 393 the `corpus-refresh-mahjong-vtt` catch-up's KI-284 row, both numbered KI-329; a re-run minutes later read `rows: 330   open: 14` with zero problems, the mahjong-vtt run having renumbered its own row to KI-330. This run met the write-side face once: its KI-316 anchor (`2154 entries / 768 dims) \| open — gen1's and agnostic-router-kit's job task strings fixed 2026-10-26`) matched at 03:49Z, and by the edit two sibling instances — blend1 03:47Z, sem1 03:49Z — had been appended ahead of it, so the anchor no longer existed (`grep -c` → 0) and the append was re-derived from a fresh read | open — the collision self-healed this time (one run noticed and renumbered); reproduced live 2026-10-28 12:44CDT — two concurrent catch-up appends both numbered KI-344 (checker flagged lines 408 and 410), self-healed by renumber within two minutes, checker green again at 348 rows; this run re-read its anchors immediately before every edit and confirmed the checker green before writing (its own append is KI-349) | the append path needs the discipline the Rust path already has: an id allocated under the ledger's own lock at write time (or stamped from a run-unique source the checker accepts), and an append that re-reads its anchor and retries instead of failing on a moved row — otherwise two concurrent catch-ups can share an id, and a row can be lost to a stale anchor |
